    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_file_raw_lo", static_cast<uint32_t>(raw));
    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_file_raw_hi", static_cast<uint32_t>(uint64_t(raw) >> 32));
    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_file_errno", static_cast<uint32_t>(err));
    bridge_copyin_credentials(phase);
}
static void bridge_copyin_directory_prepare()
{
    if (g_copyin_parent_fd >= 0) return;
    bridge_copyin_credentials(1u);
    const auto start = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
    const int flags = O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW;
    errno = 0;
    const int fd = sceKernelOpen(kProbeParent, flags, 0);
    const int err = l13_sink::error(fd, errno);
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_parent_flags", static_cast<uint32_t>(flags));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_parent_raw", static_cast<uint32_t>(fd));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_parent_errno", static_cast<uint32_t>(err));
    if (fd < 0 || err != 0) {
        bridge_copyin_file_failure(1, kProbeParent, flags, fd, err, 1);
        return;
    }
    g_copyin_parent_fd = fd;
    g_copyin_generation = static_cast<uint64_t>(std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::nanoseconds>(
        start.time_since_epoch()).count());
    g_copyin_pid = static_cast<uint32_t>(::getpid());
    g_copyin_probe_nonce = 0;
    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < kCopyinHandleLimit; ++i) {
        auto& handle = g_copyin_handles[i];
        // Target address is unknown at startup; the delayed diagnostic records
        // it separately. PID/generation/ordinal make each process-owned sink unique.
        l13_format_probe_sink(handle.path, 0, i + 1);
        const auto elapsed = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(
            std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - start).count();
        if (elapsed >= static_cast<int64_t>(kCopyinStartupBudgetMs)) {
            ::diagnostic_event("copyin_startup_timeout", static_cast<uint32_t>(elapsed));
            bridge_copyin_directory_release();
            return; // scheduling bound; cannot preempt a kernel call already entered
        }
        errno = 0;
        handle.fd = sceKernelOpen(handle.path, kCopyinSinkFlags, 0600);
        const int sink_err = l13_sink::error(handle.fd, errno);
        ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_open_flags", static_cast<uint32_t>(kCopyinSinkFlags));
        if (handle.fd < 0 || sink_err != 0) {
            bridge_copyin_file_failure(1, handle.path, kCopyinSinkFlags, handle.fd, sink_err, 1);
            handle.fd = -1;
            bridge_copyin_directory_release(); // no partial bank, no leaked owners
            return;
        }
    }
    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_startup_handles", kCopyinHandleLimit);
    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_startup_elapsed_ms", static_cast<uint32_t>(std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(
        std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - start).count()));
}
static void bridge_copyin_directory_release()
{
    for (auto& handle : g_copyin_handles) {
        if (handle.fd < 0) continue;
        errno = 0;
        const int raw = sceKernelClose(handle.fd);
        const int err = l13_sink::error(raw, errno);
        handle.fd = -1;
        if (raw != 0 || err) bridge_copyin_file_failure(4, handle.path, kCopyinSinkFlags, raw, err, 2);
    }
    if (g_copyin_parent_fd >= 0) {
        errno = 0;
        const int raw = sceKernelClose(g_copyin_parent_fd);
        const int err = l13_sink::error(raw, errno);
        g_copyin_parent_fd = -1;
        if (raw != 0 || err) bridge_copyin_file_failure(4, kProbeParent, O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW, raw, err, 2);
    }
}
static uint64_t bridge_copyin_now_ms()
{
    return static_cast<uint64_t>(std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(
        std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch()).count());
}
static void l13_format_probe_sink(char *out, uint64_t addr, uint32_t nonce)
{
    static const char prefix[] = "/data/plugins/modbridge-copyin-p";
    static const char hex[] = "0123456789abcdef";
    size_t o = 0;
    for (; prefix[o]; ++o) out[o] = prefix[o];
    for (int shift = 60; shift >= 0; shift -= 4) out[o++] = hex[(addr >> shift) & 15u];
    out[o++] = '-';
    for (int shift = 60; shift >= 0; shift -= 4) out[o++] = hex[(g_copyin_generation >> shift) & 15u];
    out[o++] = '-';
    for (int shift = 28; shift >= 0; shift -= 4) out[o++] = hex[(g_copyin_pid >> shift) & 15u];
    out[o++] = '-';
    for (int shift = 28; shift >= 0; shift -= 4) out[o++] = hex[(nonce >> shift) & 15u];
    out[o] = '\0';
}

// Control D is an explicit diagnostic entry only; module_start never runs it.
// Its address and 64-byte length are distinct from a pool-block probe.
static constexpr uint64_t kIoCtrlDCandidateAddr = 0x400000ull + 0x7dd2a40ull;
static constexpr uint64_t kIoCtrlDCandidateLen = 64u;
static uint32_t bridge_io_ld32(const unsigned char *p)
{
    return uint32_t(p[0]) | (uint32_t(p[1]) << 8) |
           (uint32_t(p[2]) << 16) | (uint32_t(p[3]) << 24);
}
static void bridge_io_canary_build(unsigned char out[32], uint64_t t_ms) noexcept
{
    out[0] = 'M'; out[1] = 'B'; out[2] = 'I'; out[3] = 'O';
    out[4] = 1; out[5] = 0; out[6] = 0; out[7] = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 8; ++i) out[8 + i] = static_cast<unsigned char>(t_ms >> (8 * i));
    for (int i = 0; i < 16; ++i)
        out[16 + i] = static_cast<unsigned char>((t_ms >> (8 * (i % 8))) ^ (0x5au + i));
}
struct BridgeProbeTelemetry { uint32_t code; uint32_t rc; uint32_t err; uint32_t sink; };
static BridgeProbeTelemetry g_last_probe_telemetry{0u, 0u, 0u, 0u};
static uint8_t g_write_copyin_capture[3][0x1000];
static uint64_t g_write_copyin_capture_addr[3];
static uint32_t g_write_copyin_capture_len[3];
static bool g_write_copyin_capture_valid[3];
static uint32_t g_write_copyin_capture_next = 0;
extern "C" int bridge_copyin_range_faulted(uint64_t addr, uint64_t len)
{
    return g_copyin_faults.overlaps(addr, len) ? 1 : 0;
}
extern "C" int bridge_write_copyin_capture(uint64_t addr, const uint8_t **out_bytes, uint32_t *out_len)
{
    if (out_bytes == nullptr || out_len == nullptr) return 0;
    *out_bytes = nullptr;
    *out_len = 0;
    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
        if (!g_write_copyin_capture_valid[i] || addr < g_write_copyin_capture_addr[i]) continue;
        const uint64_t offset = addr - g_write_copyin_capture_addr[i];
        if (offset >= g_write_copyin_capture_len[i]) continue;
        const uint32_t remaining = g_write_copyin_capture_len[i] - static_cast<uint32_t>(offset);
        if (bridge_copyin_range_faulted(addr, remaining)) return 0;
        *out_bytes = g_write_copyin_capture[i] + offset;
        *out_len = remaining;
        return 1;
    }
    return 0;
}
static int bridge_copyin_result(uint32_t code, int64_t raw, int err, uint64_t landed)
{
    g_last_probe_telemetry = {code, static_cast<uint32_t>(raw), static_cast<uint32_t>(err), static_cast<uint32_t>(landed)};
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_fresh", code);
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_rc", static_cast<uint32_t>(raw));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_errno", static_cast<uint32_t>(err));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_sink", static_cast<uint32_t>(landed));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_stop", static_cast<uint32_t>(landed));
    return code == 1 ? 1 : (code == 0 ? 0 : -1);
}

// Scheduling deadline only: no new operation begins after it. Kernel calls,
// diagnostics and mandatory descriptor cleanup cannot be preempted here.
extern "C" int bridge_kernel_write_copyin_probe_until(uint64_t addr, uint64_t len,
                                                       uint64_t deadline_ms)
{
    if (!l13_sink::valid_range(addr, len)) return bridge_copyin_result(3, 0, EINVAL, 0);
    if (bridge_copyin_range_faulted(addr, len)) return bridge_copyin_result(0, 0, EFAULT, 0);
    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
        if (g_write_copyin_capture_valid[i] && addr < g_write_copyin_capture_addr[i] + g_write_copyin_capture_len[i] &&
            g_write_copyin_capture_addr[i] < addr + len) g_write_copyin_capture_valid[i] = false;
    }
    if (bridge_copyin_now_ms() >= deadline_ms) return bridge_copyin_result(2, 0, ETIMEDOUT, 0);
    if (g_copyin_parent_fd < 0) return bridge_copyin_result(2, -1, ENOENT, 0);
    // No path lookup after startup: each fresh attempt consumes a preopened
    // exclusive sink exactly once. No cached prologue or recycled file offset.
    if (g_copyin_probe_nonce >= kCopyinHandleLimit) return bridge_copyin_result(2, 0, ENOSPC, 0);
    auto& handle = g_copyin_handles[g_copyin_probe_nonce++];
    if (handle.fd < 0) return bridge_copyin_result(2, -1, ENOENT, 0);
    const int fd = handle.fd;
    const char *probe_sink = handle.path;
    bridge_copyin_path_diagnostic(probe_sink);
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_addr_lo", static_cast<uint32_t>(addr));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_addr_hi", static_cast<uint32_t>(addr >> 32));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_len", static_cast<uint32_t>(len));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_open_flags", static_cast<uint32_t>(kCopyinSinkFlags));
    ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_open_mode", 0600u);
    ::diagnostic_event("copyin_retained_fd", static_cast<uint32_t>(fd));
    bridge_copyin_credentials(2u);
    int err = 0;
    uint64_t landed = 0;
    int64_t raw = 0;
    bool faulted = false;
    while (landed < len) {
        if (bridge_copyin_now_ms() >= deadline_ms) { err = ETIMEDOUT; break; }
        const uint64_t cursor = addr + landed;
        const uint64_t page_left = 0x1000u - (cursor & 0xfffu);
        const uint64_t chunk = len - landed < page_left ? len - landed : page_left;
        errno = 0;
        raw = sceKernelWrite(fd, reinterpret_cast<const void *>(cursor), static_cast<size_t>(chunk));
        err = l13_sink::error(raw, errno);
        if (err != 0 || raw < 0) {
            if (err == EFAULT) {
                faulted = true;
                g_copyin_faults.record(addr, len);
                ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_probe_fault_off", static_cast<uint32_t>(landed));
                ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_fault_addr_lo", static_cast<uint32_t>(cursor));
                ::diagnostic_event("pool_copyin_fault_addr_hi", static_cast<uint32_t>(cursor >> 32));
            }
            break;
        }
        if (raw == 0 || static_cast<uint64_t>(raw) > chunk) { err = EIO; break; }
        landed += static_cast<uint64_t>(raw);
        if (static_cast<uint64_t>(raw) != chunk) {
            ::diagnostic_event("pool_probe_op_rc", static_cast<uint32_t>(raw));
            ::diagnostic_event("pool_probe_stop_addr_lo", static_cast<uint32_t>(addr + landed));
            ::diagnostic_event("pool_probe_stop_addr_hi", static_cast<uint32_t>((addr + landed) >> 32));
            ::diagnostic_event("pool_probe_op_pagealign", ((addr + landed) & 0x3fffu) == 0 ? 1u : 0u);
        }
    }
    const uint32_t slot = g_write_copyin_capture_next;
    g_write_copyin_capture_valid[slot] = false;
    const uint64_t expected = len < 0x1000u ? len : 0x1000u;
    int64_t got = -1;
    if (faulted || landed != len || err != 0) {
        if (err != ETIMEDOUT) bridge_copyin_file_failure(2, probe_sink, kCopyinSinkFlags, raw, err, 2);
    } else if (bridge_copyin_now_ms() >= deadline_ms) {
        err = ETIMEDOUT;
    } else {
        errno = 0;
        // Pread does not depend on or change the write cursor. The handle was
        // opened O_RDWR at startup and is never replaced by a delayed reopen.
        got = sceKernelPread(fd, g_write_copyin_capture[slot], static_cast<size_t>(expected), 0);
        err = l13_sink::error(got, errno);
        if (err != 0 || got != static_cast<int64_t>(expected)) {
            if (!err) err = EIO;
            bridge_copyin_file_failure(3, probe_sink, kCopyinSinkFlags, got, err, 2);
        }
    }
    errno = 0;
    const int close_rc = sceKernelClose(fd);
    const int close_err = l13_sink::error(close_rc, errno);
    handle.fd = -1;
    if (close_rc != 0 || close_err) bridge_copyin_file_failure(4, probe_sink, kCopyinSinkFlags, close_rc, close_err, 2);
    if (faulted) return bridge_copyin_result(0, raw, err, landed);
    if (landed != len || err != 0) return bridge_copyin_result(2, raw, err, landed);
    if (close_rc != 0 || close_err) return bridge_copyin_result(2, close_rc, close_err, landed);
    if (bridge_copyin_now_ms() >= deadline_ms) return bridge_copyin_result(2, got, ETIMEDOUT, landed);
    g_write_copyin_capture_addr[slot] = addr;
    g_write_copyin_capture_len[slot] = static_cast<uint32_t>(got);
    g_write_copyin_capture_valid[slot] = true;
    g_write_copyin_capture_next = (slot + 1u) % 3u;
    return bridge_copyin_result(1, raw, 0, landed);
}
extern "C" int bridge_kernel_write_copyin_probe(uint64_t addr, uint64_t len)
{
    return bridge_kernel_write_copyin_probe_until(addr, len, UINT64_MAX);
}

// Legacy explicit diagnostics only. No module-start caller remains: running
// A/B/C/D eagerly would perform sink/oracle work before VirtualQuery admission.
extern "C" void bridge_io_controls(void)
{
    // Boot nonce: milliseconds of the process-steady clock — unique per
    // boot at the resolution that matters, and never derived from any
    // observable game state.
    const uint64_t t_ms = static_cast<uint64_t>(
        std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(
            std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch())
            .count());

    // ---- Control A: marker read (before any truncation) ----
    {
        unsigned char head[32];
        memset(head, 0, sizeof head);
        uint32_t a_code = 0u;
        uint32_t a_rc = 0u;
        uint32_t a_err = 0u;
        errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
        FILE *f = fopen(kSinkPath, "rb");
        if (f == nullptr) {
            a_code = 0u; // fopen failed
            a_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(errno);
            a_rc = 0xFFFFFFFFu; // no fread happened
        } else {
            errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
            const size_t got = fread(head, 1, sizeof head, f);
            a_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(errno);
            a_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(got);
            fclose(f);
            a_code = (got == sizeof head) ? 1u : ((got == 0) ? 3u : 2u);
        }
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_a", a_code);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_a_rc", a_rc);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_a_errno", a_err);
        for (int w = 0; w < 4; ++w) {
            ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_a_head", bridge_io_ld32(head + 4 * w));
        }
    }

    // ---- Control B: canary write + close-before read-back ----
    unsigned char canary[32];
    bridge_io_canary_build(canary, t_ms);
    unsigned char back[32];
    memset(back, 0, sizeof back);
    uint32_t b_code = 0u;
    uint32_t b_rc = 0u;
    uint32_t b_err = 0u;
    {
        errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
        const int wfd = sceKernelOpen(kSinkPath, O_WRONLY | O_TRUNC | O_NOFOLLOW, 0600);
        if (wfd < 0) {
            b_code = 0u; // open failed
            b_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(l13_sink::error(wfd, errno)); // error-shape law
            b_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(wfd);
        } else {
            errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
            const int64_t w = sceKernelWrite(wfd, canary, sizeof canary);
            b_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(w);
            b_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(l13_sink::error(w, errno)); // error-shape law
            errno = 0; // close is best-effort; the read-back arbitrates
            (void)sceKernelClose(wfd);
            if (w == static_cast<int64_t>(sizeof canary)) {
                errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
                FILE *rf = fopen(kSinkPath, "rb");
                if (rf == nullptr) {
                    b_code = 5u; // read-back open failed
                    b_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(errno);
                    b_rc = 0xFFFFFFFFu;
                } else {
                    errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
                    const size_t got = fread(back, 1, sizeof back, rf);
                    b_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(errno);
                    b_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(got);
                    fclose(rf);
                    if (got != sizeof back) {
                        b_code = 6u; // read-back short
                    } else {
                        b_code = (memcmp(back, canary, sizeof canary) == 0) ? 1u : 4u;
                    }
                }
            } else {
                b_code = 3u; // write short (or failed): route unproven
            }
        }
    }
    ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_b", b_code);
    ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_b_rc", b_rc);
    ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_b_errno", b_err);
    for (int w = 0; w < 4; ++w) {
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_b_readback", bridge_io_ld32(back + 4 * w));
    }

    // ---- Control C: creation test (independent sibling) ----
    {
        errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
        const int fd = sceKernelOpen(kIoCreateTestPath,
                                     O_WRONLY | O_CREAT | O_TRUNC | O_NOFOLLOW, 0600);
        uint32_t c_code = 0u;
        uint32_t c_rc = 0u;
        uint32_t c_err = 0u;
        if (fd < 0) {
            c_code = 0u; // open failed
            c_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(l13_sink::error(fd, errno)); // error-shape law
            c_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(fd);
        } else {
            errno = 0; // fresh per-operation capture
            static const unsigned char c_bytes[8] = {'M', 'B', 'C', 'R', 'E', 'A', 'T', '1'};
            const int64_t w = sceKernelWrite(fd, c_bytes, sizeof c_bytes);
            c_rc = static_cast<uint32_t>(w);
            c_err = static_cast<uint32_t>(l13_sink::error(w, errno)); // error-shape law
            errno = 0; // best-effort close: creation was decided by the open
            (void)sceKernelClose(fd);
            c_code = 1u; // created (O_CREAT open succeeded; a short write
                         // stays visible honestly in io_ctrl_c_rc)
        }
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_c", c_code);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_c_rc", c_rc);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_c_errno", c_err);
        // Best-effort cleanup of THE SIBLING only (POSIX unlink — may fail
        // in-game; non-fatal). The MAIN sink is never unlinked.
        (void)::unlink(kIoCreateTestPath);
    }

    // ---- Control D: candidate-memory copy (only when B verified) ----
    if (b_code == 1u) {
        // The probe emits its own pool_copyin_probe_* set; the io_ctrl_d_*
        // mirrors make the batched evidence set self-contained.
        (void)bridge_kernel_write_copyin_probe(kIoCtrlDCandidateAddr, kIoCtrlDCandidateLen);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_d", g_last_probe_telemetry.code);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_d_rc", g_last_probe_telemetry.rc);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_d_errno", g_last_probe_telemetry.err);
        ::diagnostic_event("io_ctrl_d_sink", g_last_probe_telemetry.sink);
    }
    // B failed: D is deliberately SKIPPED — no io_ctrl_d* tag, no probe
    // call. An unproven write route never spends the candidate copy.
}

// ---- HOST-TEST-EXTRACT-END -----------------------------------------------

// L3: the pre-lease coverage gate. Recomputes EXACTLY the ranges the
// conversion will lease — records of the same frozen snapshot that contain
// `observed_base`, are kve-readable, and intersect the extent (the adapter's
// derive_readable_ranges filter: lmf_to_native_loader_facts.cpp:81-107,
// fed from anchor_mappings built at live_module_facts.cpp:449-457) — and
// demands each one fully covered by the registry. Returns false and reports
// the gap when coverage fails; an EMPTY derivable set is NOT a gate failure
// (the conversion refuses honestly with the geometry text on its own).
static bool bridge_verify_lease_coverage(const lmf::OsVmMapping *mappings,
                                         size_t count, uint64_t observed_base,
                                         uint64_t &gap_addr,
                                         uint64_t &gap_len) noexcept
{
    const uint64_t extent_begin = kCandidateBase;
    const uint64_t extent_end = kCandidateBase + kImageExtentVaddr;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) {
        if ((mappings[i].protection & kVmProtRead) == 0) continue;
        if (!(mappings[i].start <= observed_base &&
              observed_base < mappings[i].end)) continue;
        const uint64_t b_raw = mappings[i].start < extent_begin ? extent_begin : mappings[i].start;
        const uint64_t e_raw = mappings[i].end > extent_end ? extent_end : mappings[i].end;
        if (b_raw >= e_raw) continue;
        if (bridge_lease_span_containing(b_raw, e_raw - b_raw) == nullptr) {
            gap_addr = b_raw;
            gap_len = e_raw - b_raw;
            return false;
        }
    }
    return true;
}

// L4: owned-coverage gate for the window-anchor byte reads. A span the fresh
// VMMAP reports readable is still refused unless the frozen registry covers
// it — drift between snapshots refuses loudly (fail-closed) instead of
// dereferencing unowned bytes.
static bool bridge_anchor_owned_covered(uint64_t addr, uint32_t len) noexcept
{
    if (bridge_lease_span_containing(addr, len) != nullptr) return true;
    bridge_report_lease_refusal("bridge_anchor_refuse", addr, len);
    return false;
}

// L3 state: set only inside the scan wrapper (capture), consumed once by the
// start function before conversion. Bounded printable detail for the
// existing runtime-status detail channel (copy_detail filters/caps it).
static bool g_lease_gap_reported = false;
static char g_lease_gap_detail[192];

// Bounded lowercase-hex append (no libc printf family: the packaging
// import-contract refuses new undefined imports, so the bridge hand-rolls
// what the entry's log tooling needs).
static void lease_gap_append_hex(char *out, unsigned cap, unsigned &used,
                                 uint64_t v) noexcept
{
    if (used + 2 >= cap) return;
    out[used++] = '0';
    out[used++] = 'x';
    char tmp[16];
    unsigned n = 0;
    do {
        const unsigned d = (unsigned)(v & 0xfu);
        tmp[n++] = (char)(d < 10 ? '0' + d : 'a' + d - 10);
        v >>= 4;
    } while (v != 0 && n < sizeof tmp);
    while (n != 0 && used + 1 < cap) out[used++] = tmp[--n];
}

// L3: run after the scan result is known (either route). Verifies every
// range the conversion WILL lease (frozen snapshot, records containing the
// winning chosen base, readable, clamped) against the registry; on a gap
// records the refusal for the start function's pre-conversion check.
static void bridge_gate_after_scan(const lmf::OsVmMapping *mappings,
                                   size_t count, uint64_t chosen_base) noexcept
{
    uint64_t gap_addr = 0, gap_len = 0;
    if (bridge_verify_lease_coverage(mappings, count, chosen_base,
                                     gap_addr, gap_len))
        return;
    g_lease_gap_reported = true;
    ::diagnostic_event("bridge_lease_gap", 1u);
    bridge_report_lease_refusal("bridge_lease_gap_req", gap_addr, gap_len);
    char *d = g_lease_gap_detail;
    unsigned used = 0;
    const unsigned cap = (unsigned)sizeof g_lease_gap_detail;
    const char *head = "lease coverage gap: requested [";
    for (unsigned i = 0; head[i] != '\0' && used + 1 < cap; ++i) d[used++] = head[i];
    lease_gap_append_hex(d, cap, used, gap_addr);
    const char *mid = " +0x";
    for (unsigned i = 0; mid[i] != '\0' && used + 1 < cap; ++i) d[used++] = mid[i];
    lease_gap_append_hex(d, cap, used, gap_len);
    const char *tail = ") outside registered readable spans";
    for (unsigned i = 0; tail[i] != '\0' && used + 1 < cap; ++i) d[used++] = tail[i];
    const LeaseSpan *near = nullptr;
    for (unsigned i = 0; i < g_lease_span_count; ++i) {
        if (g_lease_spans[i].b >= gap_addr + gap_len &&
            (near == nullptr || g_lease_spans[i].b < near->b))
            near = &g_lease_spans[i];
    }
    if (near != nullptr) {
        const char *mid2 = "; nearest registered [";
        for (unsigned i = 0; mid2[i] != '\0' && used + 1 < cap; ++i) d[used++] = mid2[i];
        lease_gap_append_hex(d, cap, used, near->b);
        const char *mid3 = "..";
        for (unsigned i = 0; mid3[i] != '\0' && used + 1 < cap; ++i) d[used++] = mid3[i];
        lease_gap_append_hex(d, cap, used, near->e);
        if (used + 1 < cap) d[used++] = ')';
    }
    d[used] = '\0';
}


extern "C" int modbridge_runtime_stop(void)
{
    auto &slot = owner_slot();
    if (!slot)
    {
        bridge_copyin_directory_release();
        return 0;  // nothing started; nothing to join
    }
    bool stopped = false;
    try
    {
        // Loader control thread (module_stop), never the init thread.
        // Emits at most one 'stopped' ack line, then destroys the owner.
        stopped = modbridge::native::StopRuntimeWithAck(slot);
    }
    catch (...)
    {
        slot.reset();
        // RuntimeOwner destruction stops/joins the initializer (or terminates
        // rather than returning). Only after that may its probe descriptors close.
        bridge_copyin_directory_release();
        return MRS_ERR_STOP_EXN;
    }
    if (stopped || !slot) bridge_copyin_directory_release();
    return stopped ? 0 : 1;
}

extern "C" int modbridge_runtime_start_with_activation(const char *module_file_path,
                                                       const char *mod_root,
                                                       const char *title_id,
                                                       struct mfa_activation *activation,
                                                       char *detail,
                                                       unsigned detail_capacity)
{
    auto &slot = owner_slot();
    // r13 (review P2-1, carry-over entry-review P2-2): adopt the activation
    // unconditionally and FIRST, before any early return, so the ownership
    // contract in runtime_start_bridge.h ("OWNERSHIP IS ALWAYS TRANSFERRED on
    // non-NULL input") holds on EVERY exit path including these defensive
    // refusals — a future caller trusting the header cannot leak. The
    // ActivatedMods deleter (ActivationDeleter -> mfa_release) runs on EVERY
    // exit path below, so ownership always lands in exactly one place and
    // nothing leaks on failure. A null activation (not reachable from the C
    // entry, which only calls after a successful mfa_activate) is consumed
    // and refused by the prepare call's own activation validation.
    modbridge::native::ActivatedMods mods{activation};
    if (module_file_path == nullptr || mod_root == nullptr || title_id == nullptr)
    {
        copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, "bad arguments");
        return MRS_ERR_INTERNAL;
    }
    if (slot)
    {
        copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, "runtime already started");
        return MRS_ERR_INTERNAL;
    }

    // No runtime_active bit is published on a failed start, so entry teardown
    // cannot release the retained directory. This scope closes on all empty-
    // owner exits and exceptions, while a started owner keeps its directory.
    return l13_sink::with_startup_cleanup(slot, bridge_copyin_directory_release, [&]() -> int {
    // ---- Stage 1: live module facts -> NativeLoaderFacts (noexcept capture,
    // conversion wrapper records refusals instead of throwing).
    try
    {
        // r8: register the image's readable ranges BEFORE conversion — the
        // scan source's ReadLease (inside MakeNativeProgramContext) requires
        // every readable byte pre-registered.
        // r14 (L1): that registration moved INTO the scan_image_base wrapper
        // below, so it is derived from the SAME frozen capture snapshot the
        // conversion leases from — the pre-capture fresh sysctl (r13
        // bridge_register_readable_ranges) is gone; it was the drift class
        // behind launch-8's "Owned read range includes unregistered bytes".
        lmf::LiveModuleOsOps base_ops = lmf::make_sce_live_module_os_ops(nullptr);
        // r3: wrap scan_image_base so the header scan keeps first shot; when
        // it cannot anchor (exec-only eboot header on this target), the
        // validated data-window pair anchors the image instead.
        lmf::LiveModuleOsOps ops = base_ops;
        ops.context = &base_ops;  // SCE ops ignore ctx; our wrapper reads it
        ops.query_vmentry = bridge_query_vmentry;  // r4: enlarged buffer
        // r7: serve the kernel-observed kve_path for the anchored image when
        // the sysctl KERN_PROC_PATHNAME route is empty (observed ENOENT
        // in-game); fall through to the base op otherwise.
        // r8 (user correction): the observed 144-byte VMMAP records leave only
        // 8 bytes after kve_path offset 136 — a complete path cannot fit, so
        // the kernel-observed route stays honest (NUL-in-record required) and
        // will simply not validate for these records. The fallback is an
        // EXPLICITLY CONFIGURED value (PS5-standard app mount for this title),
        // labeled as configured in diagnostics and NEVER presented as a
        // kernel-observed path. The consumer uses this only for directory/
        // configuration-root derivation; nothing depends on file existence.
        ops.executable_path = bridge_executable_path;
        ops.scan_image_base =
            [](void *ctx, const lmf::OsVmMapping *mappings, size_t count,
               lmf::VmScanResult *out) -> int {
              if (out == nullptr || mappings == nullptr) return -1;
              // r14 (L1): FIRST op of capture(), holding the FROZEN snapshot
              // the whole chain will reason over. Register every readable
              // record intersecting the anchored extent from THESE bytes —
              // never a fresh sysctl (the r13 pre-capture call is retired).
              bridge_register_from_frozen_snapshot(mappings, count);
              // spanpub (launch10 merged): publish the SAME frozen snapshot
              // for the seed-admission path (heap-inclusive, envelope-honest)
              // — same array, same moment: the same-capture rule holds.
              bridge_spanpub_publish(mappings, count, kCandidateBase, kImageExtentVaddr);
              // L16 lifecycle setup: fully open owned sinks before restoration.
              // No target reads occur here; admission still runs VirtualQuery
              // first and requests fresh copyin only when it is needed.
              bridge_copyin_directory_prepare();
              const lmf::LiveModuleOsOps &base =
                  *static_cast<const lmf::LiveModuleOsOps *>(ctx);
              // First: the original header scan (unchanged behavior).
              if (base.scan_image_base != nullptr &&
                  base.scan_image_base(nullptr, mappings, count, out) == 0 &&
                  out->chosen_index >= 0 && out->chosen_base != 0) {
                  bridge_gate_after_scan(mappings, count, out->chosen_base);
                  return 0;
              }
              // Fallback: data-window anchor (validated windows, readable
              // spans only, BOTH windows must verify at the candidate base).
              if (!window_anchor_verify()) return -1;
              lmf::VmScanResult r{};
              r.chosen_index = 0;
              r.chosen_base = kCandidateBase;
              r.chosen_extent = kImageExtentVaddr;
              r.candidate_count = 1;
              r.truncated = 0;
              *out = r;
              bridge_gate_after_scan(mappings, count, r.chosen_base);
              return 0;
            };
        auto supplier = std::make_shared<lmf::RetainedLiveModuleFactsSupplier>(
            ops, prospero_live_firmware_profile());
        supplier->capture();  // VMMAP-first; facts().status records the outcome
        bridge_capture_loaded_image(supplier->facts());
        // r14 (L3): post-capture coverage gate. Runs BEFORE conversion so a
        // gap refuses here, naming the gap and the nearest registered span,
        // instead of surfacing as a deep PLH throw inside
        // MakeNativeProgramContext (launch-8's code=2). The flag is only ever
        // set inside the scan wrapper during capture(); an empty derivable
        // set is left to the conversion's own honest geometry refusal.
        if (g_lease_gap_reported)
        {
            copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, g_lease_gap_detail);
            return MRS_ERR_CONVERSION;
        }

        // Owning conversion overload: ModuleInput.lifetime aliases the
        // supplier (and any retained loader reference) into the facts.
        const std::shared_ptr<const lmf::RetainedLiveModuleFactsSupplier> retained = supplier;
        RC::Platform::LmfConversionOptions options{};
        // In-game primary identity route (checkpoint zcode_ingame_v1_results):
        // VMMAP span + in-memory ELF base scan. Explicit opt-in, default is
        // refusal; without it a VmScan-identity snapshot never converts.
        options.allow_vm_scan_base_as_module_image_base = true;
        // Resolution B (configured roots): the observed executable path is
        // empty on this target. Converting with it EMPTY (never fabricated)
        // behind this explicit opt-in; ValidateNativeProgramContext then
        // requires configured_game_directory, set on the facts below.
        options.allow_configured_roots_for_missing_executable_path = true;

        RC::Platform::LmfConversionResult converted =  // r12: non-const — configured roots attach after conversion
            RC::Platform::TryMakeNativeLoaderFactsFromSupplier(
                retained, std::filesystem::path(module_file_path), options);
        if (!converted)
        {
            // r3 visibility: record WHICH identity route failed so the next
            // log read is unambiguous (vmentry ok? scan ran? link-map rc?).
            const lmf::LiveModuleFactsSnapshot &cap = supplier->facts();
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_vmentry_cap", (uint32_t)cap.capabilities.vmentry);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_vm_scan_cap", (uint32_t)cap.capabilities.vm_scan);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_linkmap_cap", (uint32_t)cap.capabilities.link_map_walk);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_provider_code", (uint32_t)cap.provider_code);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_identity_source", (uint32_t)cap.identity_source);
            // worker-facts-geometry-fix (controller directive, launch-9):
            // conversion-side geometry inputs through the EXISTING diagnostic
            // channel. The per-refusal PREDICATE itself is already durable:
            // each adapter refusal carries a unique static error_message that
            // copy_detail relays verbatim into the r4 UE4SS mirror line and
            // the runtime-status detail string. These four events name the
            // INPUTS that predicate saw, so a zero-interval refusal is
            // readable as "extent [base,end) vs N anchor spans" without any
            // new sink. On a refusal the extent inputs are the snapshot's
            // VmScan facts (the same numbers the window-anchor wrapper fed);
            // the 64-bit halves follow the entry's established _low/_high
            // event convention.
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_base_low",
                               (uint32_t)(cap.vm_scan.chosen_base & 0xffffffffu));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_base_high",
                               (uint32_t)(cap.vm_scan.chosen_base >> 32));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_end_low",
                               (uint32_t)((cap.vm_scan.chosen_base + cap.vm_scan.chosen_extent) & 0xffffffffu));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_end_high",
                               (uint32_t)((cap.vm_scan.chosen_base + cap.vm_scan.chosen_extent) >> 32));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_anchor_count", (uint32_t)cap.anchor_mapping_count);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_intervals_found", 0u);
            copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, converted.error_message);
            return MRS_ERR_CONVERSION;
        }

        // Resolution B: attach the EXPLICIT CONFIGURED ROOTS. mod_root is the
        // entry's MODBRIDGE_MOD_ROOT; the game directory is the configured
        // title-mount prefix. Diagnostics label them configured so a log
        // reader can always distinguish "real path served"
        // (bridge_exepath_source=1/2) from "configured roots carried"
        // (source=0 + bridge_cfg_* events).
        converted.facts.configured_game_directory = std::filesystem::path(kConfiguredGameDirectory);
        converted.facts.configured_working_directory = std::filesystem::path(mod_root);
        ::diagnostic_event("bridge_cfg_gamedir", 1u);
        ::diagnostic_event("bridge_cfg_workdir", 1u);
        // worker-facts-geometry-fix: the same geometry inputs on the SUCCESS
        // path, now from the adapter's resolved extent/provenance record, so
        // every launch logs what the intervals were derived from.
        {
            const auto &image =
                converted.facts.modules.images.at(RC::ScanTarget::MainExe);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_base_low",
                               (uint32_t)(converted.resolved_image_base & 0xffffffffu));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_base_high",
                               (uint32_t)(converted.resolved_image_base >> 32));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_end_low",
                               (uint32_t)(converted.resolved_image_end & 0xffffffffu));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_extent_end_high",
                               (uint32_t)(converted.resolved_image_end >> 32));
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_anchor_count",
                               (uint32_t)supplier->facts().anchor_mapping_count);
            ::diagnostic_event("lmf_intervals_found", (uint32_t)image.readable_ranges.size());
        }

        // ---- Stage 2: NativeProgramContext build + validation. Throws on
        // refused/invalid facts or missing regular files (both paths must
        // exist NOW, while /data is still reachable in module_start).
        try
        {
            RC::Platform::NativeProgramContext context =
                RC::Platform::MakeNativeProgramContext(std::move(converted.facts));

            // ---- Stage 3: THE composition chain, exactly as
            // INTEGRATION-CHECKLIST STEP 6 orders it:
            //   InstallDefaultEngineBindings() once,
            //   RuntimeAckConfig born from mod_root (S1/S2/S3),
            //   PrepareActivatedUE4SSRuntime (opens the single RuntimeAckSink
            //     and builds the NativeModSource from the activation),
            //   owner->start() (serialized init thread; returns immediately).
            try
            {
                slot = modbridge::native::StartActivatedRuntimeWithAck(
                    std::move(context), std::move(mods), std::string_view{title_id},
                    mod_root);
                if (!slot)
                {
                    copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity,
                                "StartActivatedRuntimeWithAck returned null owner");
                    return MRS_ERR_START;
                }
                copy_detail_or_empty(detail, detail_capacity);
                return MRS_OK;
            }
            catch (const std::exception &error)
            {
                copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, error.what());
                return MRS_ERR_START;
            }
            catch (...)
            {
                copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, "unknown exception in prepare/start");
                return MRS_ERR_START;
            }
        }
        catch (const std::exception &error)
        {
            copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, error.what());
            return MRS_ERR_CONTEXT;
        }
        catch (...)
        {
            copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, "unknown exception building program context");
            return MRS_ERR_CONTEXT;
        }
    }
    catch (const std::exception &error)
    {
        copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, error.what());
        return MRS_ERR_CONVERSION;
    }
    catch (...)
    {
        copy_detail(detail, detail_capacity, "unknown exception capturing loader facts");
        return MRS_ERR_CONVERSION;
    }
    });
}

16. INDEPENDENT FINAL LOCAL REVIEW

# Final local l16 review

2026-10-07. This supersedes the cleanup verdict in `REVIEW.md` for the source hash below. Report-only re-review: no production edits, console/account contact, uploads, payload loads or launches.

**Verdict: no remaining Important or Critical defect found in the reviewed retained-handle change. Ready for final local build/package verification. Console status remains Mac-only; upload approval remains a separate gate.**

Reviewed bridge SHA-256: `d05a307728cee0105b721934fdfed0ba739774536b89d4ad76d7fd5d4b358f00`.

## Cleanup correction

`modbridge_runtime_stop()` now releases the bank after `slot.reset()` in its exception branch and on a returned empty owner, including a negative stop result. The original failure paths were reproduced by `HOST-STOP-RED.json`; both failed assertions now pass. The initial report's P2 finding is resolved.

The selected owner source is `outputs/2026-10-04-zcode-team/worker-composition-3/bootstrap-stage/native-coherent-runtime-1/source/bootstrap/runtime_owner.cpp`, as identified by the composition compile command. Its `stop()` calls the nonthrowing backend cancellation method and joins its initializer. Its destructor calls `stop()` again and terminates if stopping cannot return successfully; it does not return with a live initializer after failed destruction. The supplier calls `owner->stop()` and resets the owner before returning. Thus normal release follows worker join, and exception-path release follows successful owner destruction/join. If destruction terminates, control cannot proceed to an unsafe release.

I independently compiled this actual selected owner source with a real host worker thread and the extracted production bridge stop and supplier stop bodies. A test seam injected an exception before supplier stop, exercising the actual owner's destructor as recovery. The close adapter checked worker completion at every descriptor close. Four cases passed in both plain and fatal ASan/UBSan builds: normal stop, injected stop exception, a negative result after actual owner destruction, and empty-owner cleanup. All had no retained descriptors and zero closes before worker completion. The acknowledgement transport was an unused host shim with a null acknowledgement sink; these tests do not establish target acknowledgement I/O or engine-hook unload safety. Temporary fixtures and binaries were removed; no project test/source/receipt was changed by this reviewer.

## Fresh verification

I freshly extracted the current production probe and stop regions into a temporary directory and compiled the current fixture twice, with the same clock-only substitution used by the checked-in runner. Both plain and AddressSanitizer/UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer builds passed all **34 assertions**, compile/run exit0, `failures=0`, no stderr. The sanitizer build used `-fno-sanitize-recover=all`. The current source hash agrees with `HOST-GREEN.json`.

The additional cases cover changed live bytes at the same address, startup UID0 observation, close-error refusal without capture publication, the startup scheduling cap, and both stop cleanup regressions. `HOST-BUDGET-RED.json` records the failing startup-budget case before the cap was added. Existing partial-write, signed/zero-extended SCE fault, exact readback, deadline, range-overflow, exhaustion, startup acquisition and owner-start cleanup cases remain green.

Startup now refuses further sink acquisition after a **5,000 ms scheduling budget** and closes a partially acquired bank. It retains at most 64 single-use sinks plus one parent. The shared **60,000 ms admission budget** remains unchanged. Neither budget can preempt an in-flight OS/native call, diagnostics or mandatory cleanup; the final sink call can finish after the scheduling deadline. The cap therefore must not be described as a hard five-second wall-clock maximum for `module_start`.

## Platform evidence and retained assumptions

The locally saved `receipts/public-api/OpenOrbis-libkernel.h` hashes to `d15c49347fabdc332e1ba180c2361e7cfe22733be2d06236262210d435ed98ea`, matching `PROTOTYPES.json`. It declares four-argument `sceKernelPread(int32_t, void*, size_t, off_t)` with a 64-bit `size_t` return on the target and `int32_t sceKernelIsInSandbox(void)`. The bridge's signed 64-bit Pread declaration consumes the same x86-64 RAX bits for error normalization; offset0 removes any offset-sign ambiguity. Sandbox results outside0/1 remain unknown/error. The UID/GID and named sysctl declarations remain supported by retained SDK headers. This public PS4 declaration evidence plus PS5 SDK export evidence is ABI evidence, not a PS5 firmware runtime proof.

Single initializer serialization remains required: the handle nonce, descriptor bank, capture ring and fault ledger have no independent locking. Normal stop joins that initializer before release. Concurrent debugger/console/control use would need explicit synchronization and lifetime ownership before being added. The current change adds no such callers.

Fresh late source-copy, exact owned prologue comparison, fault ledger, full write/read checks and refusal behavior remain intact. No startup-only/disk-only capture replaces the late check. The broader Lua I/O options remain report-only; general Lua filesystem compatibility, PC parity, controller/menu and item spawning are not established by this review.

The final two-build pipeline was still running during this re-review. Its final source pins, decoded import identities, target compile/package gates and manifests must refer to the source hash above. Initial prereview artifacts do not satisfy that requirement. PS5 startup creation, post-restoration descriptor use, added import resolution, exact live prologue match and native FName behavior remain console evidence requirements under the current mission and explicit user approval rules.

# ItemSpawner API gaps in actual runtime order

Local report only, 2026-10-07. No runtime/mod implementation, console/account contact or changes. Source: the immutable TownfallItemSpawner v1.0 Nexus release (`mod95/file218`), l15 provenance, and its API census. The user owns Townfall on PS5 only; no same-game PC reference is available.

## Verified census and stopping point

The full `API-GAPS.json` was parsed locally and its counts recomputed:

| Census category | Core/global/type surfaces | Reflected methods |
|---|---:|---:|
| Total | 17 | 33 |
| Implemented + console-proven for this downloaded mod | 0 | 0 |
| Implemented, untested | 13 | 33 |
| Missing source binding | 0 | 0 counted as missing by this source census |
| Behavior differs | 4 | 0 separately classified |

All 17 `console_proof_this_mod` fields and all 33 reflected `console_proven` fields are false. A generic reflection route being present does **not** prove any particular live game method exists, has the expected signature, or marshals successfully. “0 missing source bindings” is not a console compatibility pass and is not a claim that every possible UE4SS API exists.

The four behavior differences are `require`, `FName`, `ExecuteInGameThread`, and `RegisterLoadMapPreHook`. Each used overload/path is itself untested for this downloaded mod. The l15 census records five selected source-hash checks against the l15 receipt; that is source attribution, not runtime proof.

The recorded l15 PRX hash is `1cb1b27a2771a9178b6e5725665d398b81b56e8e84685903a67aa407b5585acb`. `main.lua` is 21,415 bytes with SHA-256 `f586043a1ca5708113e8ee31532d6396cbb0866daeae473722dd8880b6559658`; the release and harvested console copy agree in the provenance manifest. The archive SHA-256 is `a9f7ed004dd053e02f6206d8ed6886174f9b5aaecf55244447a2c5fb9d6fa7fb`. Both release files remain immutable.

Pool admission passed in l15. A delayed fresh constructor-prologue sink `_openat` then returned -1/EPERM; no source bytes were copied and the constructor was not called. Runtime refused and stopped ItemSpawner before the release's Loaded print. Consequently, registration, F7 delivery, menu creation, reflected calls and spawn have no evidence from that run. The 88-byte pure Lua diagnostic is a separate script.

Evidence: [API census JSON](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/api-audit/API-GAPS.json), [API audit narrative](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/api-audit/API-GAPS.md), [release manifest](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/provenance/MOD-RELEASE-MANIFEST.json), [l15 console result](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/CONSOLE-REPORT.md).

## Registration happens before gameplay helpers

This order is derived from calls and callbacks, not function-definition line numbers. The release defines helpers early, but defining `getPlayerController`, `scanItems`, `spawnSelected` and `buildUI` does not run them.

1. Initialize Lua tables/state and call `buildRows()` at line424. This creates row closures using ordinary Lua; it performs no object lookup, scan, UMG construction or spawn.
2. Call `RegisterKeyBind` six times, in this order: F7 (694), UP_ARROW (698), DOWN_ARROW (703), RETURN (708), LEFT_ARROW (713), RIGHT_ARROW (722). The bodies containing `ExecuteInGameThread` are deferred callbacks; they do not run during these registrations. `Key` values and later controller translation must preserve the original keys.
3. Call `pcall(RegisterLoadMapPreHook, callback)` at731. Registering successfully does not prove the callback will fire. The protected call can hide a registration error from the final Loaded message.
4. Call the release's final `log(...)` at746, which calls `print` through the helper at6.

The first required native calls **from this mod's chunk** are therefore key registration, optional map-hook registration and print, not `require`, `FindFirstOf`, `FName` or actor spawning. Runtime readiness checks can independently call native APIs before the chunk; the l15 FName witness is such a check.

## The 17 surfaces, ordered by the mod's flow

U = implemented, untested. D = behavior differs in the full API; this release's used path remains untested. Branches can skip individual calls, so this is a flow-oriented census rather than a claim that every execution has one total order.

| First relevant stage | Surface | Status | Concrete unresolved behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chunk registration | `RegisterKeyBind` | U | All six registrations, original Key constants, callback lifetime and translated PS5 delivery. |
| Chunk registration | `RegisterLoadMapPreHook` | D | Callback is stored; deferred LoadMap arm may permanently refuse delivery. Test actual map transition and UI/input cleanup. |
| Loaded / later status logging | `print` | U | No Loaded print from the downloaded mod in l15. Later scan/menu/spawn status/error logs also use it. |
| F7, navigation, Enter callbacks | `ExecuteInGameThread` | D | Prospero uses a FuncPtr-marshal carrier instead of the normal PC queue path. Prove carrier readiness, bounded refusal, ordering and actual engine-thread identity. |
| First F7 item scan | `FindFirstOf` | U | First finds ItemDatabaseSubsystem. Later controller fallback finds PlayerController. Real class/name/object-array traversal is untested. |
| Scan and every live-object operation | `UObject:IsValid` | U | Real game validity/lifetime, invalid-wrapper behavior and stale references. Nil checks can skip invocation. |
| Scan method lookup; UI and spawn properties | `UObject.__index / __newindex` | U | Reflected function lookup, reads and writes use exact game offsets and ownership. Needs live data validation. |
| Database item/path conversion; later inventory ID | `FName/FText/FString:ToString` | U | `str()` bypasses ToString for an ordinary Lua string; non-string native values need actual conversion proof. Pool anchor admission does not prove it. |
| F7 UI controller lookup, then repeated lookups | `require` | D | `pcall(require, "UEHelpers")` occurs inside `getPlayerController`, not at chunk load. Native loader keeps preload plus retained-buffer searcher and removes ordinary disk/C searchers. This import and package caching are untested. |
| First UEHelpers import / invalid-controller fallback | `CreateInvalidObject` | U | UEHelpers module initialization creates EngineCache and GameInstanceCache invalid wrappers. GetPlayerController can return another invalid wrapper. |
| UEHelpers.GetPlayerController | `FindAllOf` | U | Enumerates PlayerController; tries Controller only if the first result is falsy, not merely an empty table. Returned objects/controller selection are untested. |
| F7 UMG lookup and construction; later trace/assets | `StaticFindObject` | U | Local traversal/path matching rather than a verified native StaticFindObject function. Real UMG class/default-object and cooked asset lookup need proof. |
| UMG construction arguments | `FName` | D | Release uses default Add1. Corrected output/UTF16 ABI is host-tested and never called in l15. Full API accepts Replace2 in Lua but native adapter rejects it. |
| UMG tree/widgets and root fallback | `StaticConstructObject` | U | Parameters layout, outer/name handling and game constructor ABI. Root fallback is conditional; later WidgetTree/CanvasPanel/Border/TextBlock construction is required on the normal build path. |
| Controller collections, UMG parameters/properties, spawn trace | `UScriptStruct / array / OUT marshalling` | U | Array/controller paths, anchors, vectors, colors, margins, fonts and nested writable properties; later Rotator/Transform/HitResult and 12-argument trace OUT semantics. |
| UMG text creation / refresh | `FText` | U | Native construction, text ownership and SetText/property copying. |
| Spawn class absent from live objects | `LoadAsset` | U | Conditional asset-load fallback followed by another StaticFindObject; true game-thread and live asset-service behavior are untested. |

There are no missing source-binding entries to list. Any live absence or refused dispatch encountered later must be recorded as an actual failure rather than inferred from symbol presence.

## F7: scan first, then build and show the original UMG menu

The first F7 callback calls `ExecuteInGameThread(toggleMenu)`. With the initial `menuOpen=false`, `toggleMenu` calls `showMenu(true)`. When the item list is empty, **`scanItems()` runs before `buildUI()`**:

1. `database()` calls `FindFirstOf("ItemDatabaseSubsystem")` and checks IsValid. The scan adds the six cooked-class entries, then queries legacy enums through `GetItemIDFromDeprecatedEnum` and, only for a nonempty/non-None ID, `GetInteractablePickupIDActorPath`. Native results go through `str`/ToString; the Lua list is sorted and status is logged. A failed scan is protected and does not automatically prevent `buildUI` from being attempted.
2. `buildUI()` checks its existing UI state, then calls `getPlayerController()`. The first successful UEHelpers import executes its two cache initializers. GetPlayerController uses `FindAllOf`, IsValid, the `IsPlayerController` property/method and an OR fallback to `IsLocalPlayerController`; the release then validates the result. If the helper path fails, the release tries `FindFirstOf("PlayerController")`.
3. Find the WidgetBlueprintLibrary default object and UserWidget class. Try its reflected `Create(pc, class, pc)`. Only if that fails/returns invalid, construct the root with `StaticConstructObject(..., FName("TownfallItemSpawnerRoot"))`.
4. Build WidgetTree, CanvasPanel, frame Border, inner Border and VerticalBox; assign `ui.root.WidgetTree` and `ui.tree.RootWidget`; add children and set layout/content/style. `construct()` evaluates `class(path)` and `FName(name)` before calling StaticConstructObject. It is not enough to expose an alternative runtime-owned PS5 menu.
5. Repeated `addStyledText` calls construct TextBlocks, AddChild, set padding/text/font/color/wrapping/shadow. Build section labels and rows, refresh text, then `AddToViewport(9999)` and initial `SetVisibility(1)`. Log Menu created.
6. Back in `showMenu`, refresh again, `SetVisibility(0)`, look up the controller again, call `SetIgnoreLookInput(true)` and `SetIgnoreMoveInput(true)`, then set `menuOpen=true`.

Several styling, visibility and input-lock calls are wrapped in `pcall`. A Menu created print or `buildUI()` true result can coexist with silently failed optional calls. Acceptance must show the original menu visibly, readable labels and selection, correct input locking, close/reopen behavior, and no orphaned widgets/input locks.

UP/DOWN callbacks change selection and refresh. LEFT/RIGHT change pages and refresh. RETURN calls `ExecuteInGameThread(activateSelection)` while the menu is open. The quantity/page/refresh rows perform their own actions; **only a selected pickup row calls `spawnSelected(entry)`**. Empty pickups can return without spawning. Closing with F7 hides the root and clears the input locks.

## All 33 reflected methods, in flow order

Every method below is U. The generic UObject/UFunction dispatch exists in source; live method existence, parameter layouts, return values and successful invocation are untested. Repeated names appear once here at their first relevant stage; controller/helper methods and UI refresh recur later.

| Stage | Methods in call-flow order | Important branch/layout condition |
|---|---|---|
| First F7 database scan | `GetItemIDFromDeprecatedEnum`, `GetInteractablePickupIDActorPath` | Second call only after a valid ID; strings/path data must match the live database. |
| UI controller helper | `IsPlayerController`, `IsLocalPlayerController` | Property existence and Lua OR short-circuit determine which methods run. |
| UMG root | `Create` | Invalid/error result selects the StaticConstructObject fallback. |
| UMG panel tree/layout | `AddChild`, `SetAnchors`, `SetAlignment`, `SetPosition`, `SetSize`, `SetPadding`, `SetBrushColor`, `SetContent` | Child slot class, anchors/vectors/margins/colors and ownership must be correct. Later TextBlock additions reuse AddChild/SetPadding. |
| Text and row styling | `SetText`, `SetColorAndOpacity`, `SetAutoWrapText`, `SetShadowColorAndOpacity` | FText/color representation plus Font.Size/ShadowOffset property writes; pcall can hide style failure. |
| Display and input lock | `AddToViewport`, `SetVisibility`, `SetIgnoreLookInput`, `SetIgnoreMoveInput` | Expected visibility0/1, viewport order9999, actual controller state and clean unlock. |
| Spawn player/pawn lookup | `GetPawn`, `K2_GetPawn` | Try pc.Pawn property first; each method is a subsequent fallback only if earlier pawn candidates fail validation. |
| Spawn geometry and ground trace | `K2_GetActorLocation`, `GetControlRotation`, `LineTraceSingle` | Vector/Rotator read, 12 arguments, actor array and HitResult OUT table. Require a valid hit point and sufficiently flat normal. |
| Actor creation | `BeginDeferredActorSpawnFromClass`, `FinishSpawningActor` | Real game thread, complete Transform and successful native actor lifetime. |
| Pickup component fallback | `GetComponentByClass` | Try actor.PickupInteractable property first; call only if that is invalid. |
| Configure valid pickup | `SetEnabled`, `SetActorHiddenInGame`, `SetActorEnableCollision` | Nested inventory ID must be valid and match the requested database ID before these calls. |
| Failed-spawn cleanup | `K2_DestroyActor` | Conditional on an actor surviving failure with IsValid true. |

The grouped list contains all 33 names from the JSON census exactly once. Helper-only IsPlayerController/IsLocalPlayerController are included. Unrelated UEHelpers exports (`ModRef:GetSharedVariable`, `ModRef:SetSharedVariable`, `UnrealVersion:IsBelow`, weak-object helpers) are not reached by this mod's helper path and are not added to the count.

## Enter: exact spawn chain and cleanup

`activateSelection()` invokes the chosen row's closure and refreshes after it. A pickup closure indexes the current page's entry and calls `spawnSelected`. That function guards busy/empty entry, marks busy, and runs this protected chain:

1. `getPlayerPawn()` calls `getPlayerController()` and validates it, then tries `pc.Pawn`, `pc:GetPawn()`, `pc:K2_GetPawn()` in that order, stopping at the first valid pawn. It then looks up and validates the controller again.
2. Call pawn K2_GetActorLocation and controller GetControlRotation; read position X/Y/Z and yaw. Find KismetSystemLibrary and LineTraceSingle forward/downward with the pawn ignored. Read `hit.ImpactPoint or hit.Location`, optional ImpactNormal, and reject missing ground/steep surface.
3. Build Transform from the resulting ground point. Normalize the entry's cooked class path; StaticFindObject; if invalid, LoadAsset followed by StaticFindObject again; refuse if still invalid.
4. Find GameplayStatics; BeginDeferredActorSpawnFromClass, validate the actor, then FinishSpawningActor. Locate PickupInteractable directly or through GetComponentByClass.
5. Read `pickup.PickupInventoryItem.ItemId` and convert it. Refuse an empty/None ID or mismatch with a nonempty requested ID. Write `PickupAmount` and `bAutoDestroyOnPickUp`, then SetEnabled, SetActorHiddenInGame and SetActorEnableCollision. Retain the actor in ownedActors and log success.
6. Clear busy regardless of the protected call's result. On failure, log its stage and conditionally K2_DestroyActor. The outer selection action then refreshes the status text.

The map-prehook can fire at a later transition, outside the above linear path. It closes an open menu, unlocks input through `showMenu(false)`, clears UI/item/ownedActors references, and resets selection/busy/status. It does not individually destroy all retained actors. Test real callback delivery and safe map transition rather than equating registration with working cleanup.

## Validation order and limits

Keep the requested sequence: resolve the live witness refusal → off-by-default runtime tooling → runtime/config controller translation to the original PC keys → original mod UMG menu → an actual pickup spawn → conformance and more unmodified mods → deferred B/C/A phases. Do not edit the PC release or substitute a proxy menu to satisfy the ladder.

Conformance must record deterministic case IDs, normalized values, explicit failed/skipped reasons, real callback-thread proof, data/layout validation, and cleanup/revert for widget/map/spawn cases. A same-game PC comparison requires an otherwise authorized reference run; another UE5.6 title can examine generic APIs but cannot establish Townfall-specific database, cooked pickup or UMG parity. Host tests, bound symbols, source-hash checks and Ploader OK do not fill that gap. No conformance script or diff tool was installed or run for this report.

18. UNIVERSAL LATE FILE-IO OPTIONS — REPORT ONLY

# Post-startup file I/O for unmodified Lua mods — local investigation

Report only, 2026-10-07. No runtime implementation, mod edits, console contact, account contact, uploads, configuration changes, payload loads, or launches were performed. The user-approved l16 work is the bounded startup-opened probe-handle repair. This report describes broader options; it does not extend that approval.

## Recommendation

Keep l16 narrowly scoped to fully opened probe files. For broader pure-Lua mod file compatibility, use one runtime-owned logical filesystem with the existing immutable release snapshot as its lower layer and a separate writable copy-on-write layer. First establish whether the game has a usable post-startup writable root. If it does, persist that layer there. If late physical creation remains denied, evaluate a startup-opened journal/container or an outside-game file broker behind the same interface. A memory-only layer can support transient reads/writes, but must disclose that it does not provide persistence across launches.

Keep kstuff-assisted changes to game credentials/sandbox state deferred to Phase B. The present log does not establish that a kernel change is necessary. Neither a retained directory descriptor nor a finite set of preopened ordinary files gives arbitrary future filenames physical backing.

No option is yet console-proven as a general Lua I/O solution. “Unmodified Lua mod compatibility” should mean documented Lua operations over configured mod/config/data paths, with explicit errors for unsupported resources. Arbitrary Windows drive paths, native PC DLL modules, subprocess execution, and host-specific Lua extensions need separate compatibility work; file I/O alone cannot make every PC mod portable.

## What l15 actually observed

The finished l15 harvest belongs to Townfall PID108. Pool admission passed all 13 checks in 22 ms with a shared 60,000 ms budget. The subsequent constructor prologue witness was unavailable because its sink creation failed. The native FName constructor was never called and the downloaded mod did not reach its Loaded print.

| Event | Exact recorded result |
|---|---|
| Startup parent directory open | fd19, `t_ms=146524` |
| `module_start` return | `t_ms=146623` |
| Fresh delayed prologue request | address `0x63D590`, length32 |
| Delayed `_openat` creation | `t_ms=146728`, raw `0xffffffff` / signed -1, errno1 / EPERM |
| Arguments | dirfd19; flags `0xF01`, including O_CREAT; mode `0600` |
| Sink | `/data/plugins/modbridge-copyin-p000000000063d590-000000221dd1c466-0000006c-00000001` |
| Copied sink bytes | 0; no source-memory copy occurred |

The file creation occurred 105 ms after `module_start` returned. Retaining the parent directory did not make it succeed. This is transport-policy evidence, not an EFAULT, signature mismatch, bad FName ABI, or deadline expiry. Restored loader credentials, sandbox policy, and the delayed POSIX route are plausible causes; the exact cause remains unproved. The earlier startup parent open alone does not prove startup creation of a read/write file or delayed use of that file will succeed.

This does **not** establish that every Lua `io.open` fails. The failing operation was a particular `_openat` creation at a particular path, flags, process, and time. The downloaded ItemSpawner contains no direct `io.open`, `io.lines`, `loadfile`, or `dofile` call. Its deferred `require("UEHelpers")` uses the retained-buffer loader. The separate 88-byte buffered Lua diagnostic proves neither this mod nor physical file I/O. Existing-file reads, another allowed root, read-only opens, the public `fopen` route, Sony-native opens, and use of startup-opened file descriptors must be tested separately.

Evidence: [l15 console report](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/CONSOLE-REPORT.md), [current-launch raw events](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/console-test-20261007T170125Z-b1b2fd00/harvest/out-1791392871-61504/supplier-current-launch.jsonl:574). The event timestamps and errno were checked locally against those raw events.

## Existing ownership and loader boundaries

`MakeActivatedUE4SSModSource` consumes the startup activation, moves it into a shared owner, and aliases its already preloaded bytes into `NativeFileBuffer` views. It supplies files without discovering engine addresses or doing new filesystem access. Every returned view retains the activation; the owner cannot be released while parsing or a file handle still borrows those bytes. See [activation adapter](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/.superpowers/native-dependency-spike/native-coherent-runtime-1/source/bootstrap/activated_mod_source.cpp:18).

`NativeModSource` is a final class representing a bounded immutable snapshot, not a mutable filesystem. Keys are canonical UTF-8 logical names below `ue4ss/`. It rejects absolute paths, backslashes/colons, control characters, invalid UTF-8, empty components, `.` and `..`. It bounds the snapshot to 4,096 files, 8 MiB per file and 64 MiB total. `read()` returns an owned view or absence; there is no open/create/write/seek/rename/remove API. See [buffer contract](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/.superpowers/native-dependency-spike/native-coherent-runtime-1/source/port/ue4ss/include/Platform/NativeModSource.hpp:10) and [validation implementation](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/.superpowers/native-dependency-spike/native-coherent-runtime-1/source/port/ue4ss/src/Platform/NativeModSource.cpp:48).

The integrated `LuaMod.cpp` native branch keeps package.preload and its buffer searcher, replaces `loadfile`/`dofile`, and loads `main.lua` from a retained `NativeFileBuffer`. Relative file-loader names currently resolve under that mod's Scripts directory; the buffer module searcher checks Scripts and shared-module candidates. Standard Lua/C disk searchers are removed. This is a deliberate compatibility boundary, not proof of general `io` support. See [native loader setup](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-04-zcode-team/worker-l11-merge/staging/luamod-l11-merged/LuaMod.cpp:1179), [buffer file resolution](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-04-zcode-team/worker-l11-merge/staging/luamod-l11-merged/LuaMod.cpp:794), and [main-script loading](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-04-zcode-team/worker-l11-merge/staging/luamod-l11-merged/LuaMod.cpp:7315).

The stdio consumer adaptation is integrated according to the l15 transport ledger, but its `liolib.c` `io_open` still calls `fopen(filename, mode)` and returns Lua file errors on failure. The change avoids SDK FILE macros; it does not add a virtual writable layer. This is why successful buffer loading and general late physical `io.open` must remain separate claims. See [adapted Lua I/O](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/.superpowers/native-dependency-spike/ue4ss-stdio-consumer-adaptation-1/patched/liolib.c:270) and [integration receipt](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-07-l15-full-build/receipts/prx-candidate-l15-2/driver/transport-ledger.composition6-mirror.json:5282).

## Options and tradeoffs

| Option | How an unmodified mod would use it | Main limits and risks | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Redirect to a sandbox-writable root | Runtime resolves the mod's logical relative paths into a configured, game-legal physical writable tree; Lua code remains unchanged. | No suitable late writable root has been proved here. File ownership, title isolation, cwd expectations, text/binary modes, rename/flush behavior and wrapper ABI still matter. A raw directory fd does not itself grant creation rights. | Prefer the simplest physical backend if a focused late-I/O matrix proves it. Do not guess a Sony mount path. |
| Virtual filesystem with immutable lower layer and writable upper layer | Lua file handles read release data from retained buffers; new data/config files and writes are represented in an upper layer. A virtual write to a release logical name creates a private copy and leaves release bytes/hash unchanged. | Must implement actual file semantics, not just replace `io.open`. Memory quotas, coherent concurrent handles, EOF/error behavior, persistence and lifetime accounting require work. A transient overlay is not a persistent filesystem. | Recommended reusable runtime architecture. Reuse the immutable owner's lifetime contract and add a separate mutable owner. |
| Startup-opened journal/container for VFS persistence | Arbitrary virtual filenames are stored as records in a bounded file opened read/write during startup; later reads/writes use the same handle. | Delayed read/write/seek/truncate/fsync are unproved. Requires a serialized format, crash recovery, capacity checks and atomic commit discipline. Reopening the file later recreates the original problem. Physical rename/remove cannot be assumed. | Worth evaluating after retained-handle behavior is proved. A finite preopen pool without a container only covers known files. |
| Privileged outside-game broker | Lua file requests use the same runtime filesystem interface; a separate authorized console process performs open/create/read/write/rename/remove and returns bytes or errors. It does not execute mods or call Unreal. | Transport availability after startup is unproved; neither a socket nor shared memory route is automatically allowed. Requires process/session identity, path confinement, protocol bounds, timeouts, cancellation, handle cleanup and durable flush acknowledgements. Blocking the game thread on broker I/O risks stalls; a broker crash must fail cleanly. | Most general persistence fallback without changing game credentials, but a substantial new service and separately authorized deployment. Avoid assuming FTP is a secure or suitable runtime RPC channel. |
| kstuff-assisted credential/sandbox access | A firmware-matched, reversible mechanism could make a physical backend usable when policy denies it. | Root credentials may still be insufficient for Sony sandbox policy; cause and offsets are unproved. Credential changes can race across threads, broaden the whole game and affect every title depending on kstuff. Kernel writes/recovery are higher risk than a file service. | Deferred Phase B only. No kernel change is justified or authorized by this report. |

The FreeBSD description distinguishes `openat` path resolution from a returned file descriptor and documents creation flags; it does not establish PS5 policy or grant a retained directory new privileges. The platform inference here remains conditional. [FreeBSD open/openat manual](https://man.freebsd.org/cgi/man.cgi?query=open&sektion=2)

The public kstuff-lite repository describes a `kekcall` path and checked kernel-memory operations. That is not evidence that this PS5 firmware permits safe file-policy changes. The local mission requires checking the existing interface first and using firmware-matched kstuff-lite rather than quarantined kstuffSharp/InstallerApp. [Upstream kstuff-lite](https://github.com/EchoStretch/kstuff-lite)

## Concrete runtime design requirements, not implementation

Preserve `NativeModSource` as the immutable lower layer. Add a separately owned virtual file service associated with the runtime/mod context, with logical handles carrying mode, cursor, closed/error state and lifetime. A lower-layer reader retains its `NativeFileBuffer`; a writer owns an upper-layer copy. Do not mutate the activation buffer or use its `string_view` as writable storage. Shutdown must stop users and settle/cancel I/O before destroying backend/session resources; Lua finalizers and errors must not bypass C++ cleanup.

Route `io.open` and filename-based implicit I/O through the same resolver as `loadfile`, `dofile` and the Lua module searcher, so read-after-write and newly created modules are coherent. A backend wrapper around physical `fopen` alone would leave the existing buffer-only loader unable to see new script/data files. Retain the original release manifest as the audit baseline and record upper-layer state separately.

The compatibility checklist is read/write/append/update modes, binary bytes, cursor/seek, line and numeric reads, write results, flush/close, implicit input/output, lines iterators, `io.type`, finalization and close behavior, temporary files, and normal error tuples. `os.rename`/`os.remove` and loader visibility must use the same path policy. Standard Lua permits customized module searchers. These are acceptance targets, not claims about the present runtime. [Lua 5.4 I/O and modules](https://www.lua.org/manual/5.4/manual.html#6.8)

Resolve PC-style path spelling at the runtime boundary before canonical internal validation: configured separators/root aliases, relative-base semantics, case handling and approved absolute aliases. Keep game/build roots in profiles and portable mechanism code independent of Townfall. Explicitly refuse paths outside configured roots and unsupported C-library loading/subprocesses; silently succeeding without persistence or silently falling back outside the mapped tree would mislead mods.

## Future evidence needed

These are proposed tests only; no console test was run for this report.

1. For l16, prove startup game-created read/write file opens, then a **fresh** delayed 32-byte source copy, complete same-handle readback and exact prefix match. Record raw return/errno for every open/write/read/seek/close operation. Test short transfer, EFAULT, consumed handle, timeout, refusal and cleanup. No cached-prefix or disk-only substitution may pass.
2. For broader I/O, compare startup and delayed execution on the same process using explicit APIs: `_openat`, public `fopen`/Lua `io.open`, and Sony-native calls. Separately test existing-file read, existing-file update and new-file creation at each approved candidate root. Use game-created files and record ownership/modes; do not confuse FTP-created permissions with game-created permissions.
3. Test a fully retained file handle after startup for ordinary safe-buffer write, seek, read, truncate if required, flush and close. Prove persistence in a later launch. The l16 memory-copy witness and ordinary Lua I/O have different purposes; neither replaces the other.
4. Conformance cases should exercise the semantic checklist, multiple handles, shared and per-mod paths, upper-layer changes seen by loaders, malformed/outside-root paths, quota exhaustion and restart recovery. Keep downloaded mod files immutable. Report failed/skipped cases explicitly.
5. If a broker is chosen, prove late transport access, timeouts/disconnect recovery, isolation between title/PID/session generations, durable writes and cleanup before calling it a persistent backend. Do not couple Lua file requests to engine calls.

The preserved mission order remains refusal resolution → off-by-default runtime tooling → controller-to-original-PC-key translation → the original mod's UMG menu → item spawn → conformance and more unmodified mods → deferred B/C/A capability phases. Phase B begins only after a PS5 item spawn, needs per-step user approval, save backups before kernel tests, a proved recovery route, read-only kernel work before writes, and three logged passes across reboots. kstuffSharp/InstallerApp remains quarantined; game executable patching remains suspended. [Mission](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-06-recovery-fakesign/PS5-MODDING-MISSION.md), [standing recovery rules](/WORKSPACE/.codex/worktrees/translation-core/what/outputs/2026-10-06-recovery-fakesign/POSTMORTEM.md).

The user owns Townfall on PS5 only. There is no same-game PC reference run; host tests and standard-Lua tests can narrow defects but cannot establish Townfall PC parity.
